<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD12-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm69" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>x</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>y</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>θ</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>y</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable columnalign="center"><mtr><mtd><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>−</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>θ</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>sin</mi><mi>θ</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(12)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula: <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm70" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo> </mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo> </mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi>T</mi></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the position of the ferry vehicle in the <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm71" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>-time world coordinate system; <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm72" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo> </mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo> </mo><msub><mi>θ</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi>T</mi></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the posture of the ferry vehicle in the <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm73" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></math></span> time world coordinate system; <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm74" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi><mo> </mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi><mo> </mo><msub><mi>ω</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mi>T</mi></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the position and attitude change under the <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm75" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></math></span> time ferry bus coordinates.</p></section><section id="sec4dot1dot2-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.1.2. Driverless Ferry Vehicle Speed Sampling Space</h4>
<p>In the actual driving process of the vehicle, its speed is related to the inherent mechanisms of the vehicle, such as the motor and brake. At the same time, the obstacles and road boundaries in the driving environment also limit the speed, so in the actual speed sampling process, it is necessary to sample in the speed window that meets certain constraints. The speed sampling space diagram of the vehicle during driving is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f011" class="usa-link">Figure 11</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f011"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 11.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g011.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/d6dbe8a69107/sensors-24-04041-g011.jpg" loading="lazy" height="315" width="685" alt="Figure 11"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f011/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Speed sampling space of ferry vehicles.</p></figcaption></figure><p>Combined with the above figure, the speed sampling space is analyzed, and the conditions that limit the vehicle speed and angular speed mainly include the following:</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none"><li>
<span class="label">(1)</span><p class="display-inline">Kinematic constraint</p>
</li></ul>
<p>Affected by dynamic performance and other key factors, the expression of the maximum and minimum speed range of the vehicle is as follows:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD13-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm76" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>≤</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>ω</mi><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub><mo>≤</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>ω</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(13)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>Among them, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm77" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm78" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> represent the minimum speed and maximum speed of the vehicle, respectively, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm79" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span>. <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm80" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>ω</mi><mrow><mi>min</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm81" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>ω</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> represent the minimum angular speed and maximum angular speed of the vehicle, respectively, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm82" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>rad</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none"><li>
<span class="label">(2)</span><p class="display-inline">Dynamic constraint</p>
</li></ul>
<p>The vehicle is limited by the performance of the acceleration and braking mechanisms [<a href="#B23-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B23-sensors-24-04041">23</a>]. When the vehicle accelerates or brakes at maximum linear acceleration or turns at maximum angular acceleration or deceleration, the dynamic speed sampling space of the vehicle can be obtained as shown in the following formula:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD14-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm83" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>v</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>v</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>a</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>ω</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>≤</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>≤</mo><msub><mi>ω</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>a</mi></msub><mo>Δ</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(14)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>
where <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm84" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the frontline speed of the vehicle, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm85" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span>; <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm86" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>v</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>a</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm87" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>v</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> are the maximum acceleration and deceleration of the vehicle in the course of driving, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm88" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span>. <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm89" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>ω</mi><mi>c</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the current angular speed of the vehicle, in <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm90" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>rad</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span> units; <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm91" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>a</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm92" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> are the maximum angular acceleration and deceleration of the vehicle, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm93" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mrow><mi>rad</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none"><li>
<span class="label">(3)</span><p class="display-inline">Security constraint</p>
</li></ul>
<p>In order to ensure the safety of the vehicle, it is necessary to control the vehicle to stop moving before the nearest obstacle collides, so the speed range is further reduced to Formula (15):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD15-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm94" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>a</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>≤</mo><msqrt><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>v</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub></mrow></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>≤</mo><msqrt><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>⋅</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>⋅</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>ω</mi><mo>˙</mo></mover><mi>b</mi></msub></mrow></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(15)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm95" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the nearest distance to the obstacle on the corresponding trajectory of the velocity vector <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm96" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<p>Combining the above three speed constraints, the final selectable velocity sampling space is the intersection of the above three sets, so that <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm97" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> represents the allowable velocity set; then, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm98" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> should satisfy the following:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD16-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm99" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>m</mi></msub><mo>∩</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>d</mi></msub><mo>∩</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>a</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(16)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>As shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f011" class="usa-link">Figure 11</a> above, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm100" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the intersection of the above different speed ranges. When the vehicle carries out speed sampling, it can only sample in the shadow space, that is, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm101" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> areas. In the continuous speed space <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm102" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm103" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span> is converted into discrete points according to the sampling points of the speed. The trajectory of the sampling points is predicted according to the dynamic characteristics of the vehicle, and multiple predicted trajectories at the next time, as shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f012" class="usa-link">Figure 12</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f012"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 12.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><img class="graphic" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/ab0204254d7f/sensors-24-04041-g012.jpg" loading="lazy" height="601" width="787" alt="Figure 12"></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f012/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Trajectory prediction map.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The vehicle will score the multiple paths generated by the above image based on the trajectory evaluation function and select the optimal track for output.</p></section><section id="sec4dot1dot3-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.1.3. Trajectory Evaluation Function</h4>
<p>Many tracks generated in the current speed sampling space are drivable paths for vehicles, but vehicles are usually expected to follow the most ideal path in actual driving. The optimal driving path should meet the following requirements:</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none">
<li>
<span class="label">(1)</span><p class="display-inline">The planned path can reach the target point.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(2)</span><p class="display-inline">The planned path should be kept at a safe distance from obstacles.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(3)</span><p class="display-inline">The speed of the vehicle should be kept at a high level within the allowable range.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>Based on the above requirements, the evaluation function of the traditional DWA is shown in Formula (17):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD17-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm104" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>α</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>β</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>γ</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(17)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm105" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the heading angle evaluation factor between the different track points and the target points of the vehicle in the current planning cycle, and the calculation formula is shown in Formula (18):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD18-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm106" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>180</mn></mrow><mo>∘</mo></msup><mo>−</mo><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(18)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm107" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></math></span> is the angle between the end of the predicted track of the vehicle and the connection line of the target point, in degrees. The smaller the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm108" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></math></span> is, the more inclined the mobile robot is to move towards the target point, and the larger the target azimuth evaluation function is.</p>
<p><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm109" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the evaluation factor of the distance between the different tracks of the vehicle and the nearest obstacle in the current planning cycle, as shown in Formula (19):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD19-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm110" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="" open="{"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mi>d</mi><mo>,</mo><mtext>     </mtext><mi>d</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mtext>  </mtext><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(19)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm111" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></math></span> is the distance from the point on the track of the vehicle to the nearest obstacle, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm112" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the threshold of the distance from the vehicle to the obstacle. The larger the distance evaluation factor is, the farther the vehicle is from the obstacle on the predicted trajectory, and the higher the safety of the vehicle. The function of the formula <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm113" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mrow><mi>max</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is to ensure that there are no obstacles on a certain simulation trajectory, which leads to a too large obstacle distance evaluation factor and a too large proportion in the total trajectory evaluation function.</p>
<p><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm114" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the speed evaluation factor of the vehicle in the current planning cycle, as shown in Formula (20):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD20-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm115" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>s</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>s</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(20)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formula, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm116" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> represents the absolute value of the parameter, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm117" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>s</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is the linear speed corresponding to the predicted track of the vehicle, in units of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm118" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span>. The higher the value of the speed evaluation factor, the faster the vehicle can approach the target point on the simulation track, but too much speed will also cause the vehicle to miss the target point when it is very close to the target point.</p>
<p><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm119" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm120" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm121" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> represent the weight coefficients of different evaluation factors, respectively, and the range of values is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm122" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></math></span>. Because the dimensions of the three evaluation factors are different, in order to avoid the excessive impact of one evaluation factor on the results, thus weakening the role of other evaluation factors, it is necessary to normalize the above evaluation factors. The normalized calculation formulas for different evaluation factors are shown in the following Formulas (21) to (23):</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD21-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm123" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>n</mi></munderover><mrow><mi>h</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>…</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(21)</td>
</tr></table>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD22-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm124" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>n</mi></munderover><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>…</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(22)</td>
</tr></table>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD23-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm125" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><munderover><mo>∑</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mi>n</mi></munderover><mrow><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>y</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>…</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(23)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>In the formulae, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm126" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow></math></span> is the total number of predicted trajectories in the current velocity sampling space, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm127" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow></math></span> is the current predicted trajectory to be evaluated, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm128" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> represents the normalized parameters. In summary, by calculating the value of the trajectory evaluation function of each predicted trajectory, the track with the highest score is selected, that is, the optimal trajectory.</p></section><section id="sec4dot1dot4-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.1.4. Problems with the DWA</h4>
<p>Through the above analysis of the principle of the DWA, we can see that the selection of the optimal trajectory is realized by adjusting the three weight coefficients of the trajectory evaluation function. The traditional DWA uses a fixed weight coefficient for path planning, but it is obviously not appropriate to use a constant weight coefficient of the evaluation function for path planning in different environments, so how to determine the values of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm129" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm130" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm131" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> is very important. At present, there is no detailed reference on how to select the weight coefficients <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm132" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm133" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm134" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>. In order to make the driverless ferry vehicle complete the autonomous navigation task in the complex dynamic scene, the local path planning algorithm needs to have certain flexibility. For the driverless ferry vehicle that uses the traditional dynamic window method for local path planning, if the weight coefficient in the trajectory evaluation function is constant, the navigation ability in the complex dynamic scene will decline, and the quality of the planned path will not be high [<a href="#B24-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B24-sensors-24-04041">24</a>]. In this paper, the problems and defects of the traditional DWA are analyzed through simulation experiments.</p>
<p>The setting parameters for the environment and ferry vehicle are as follows: the map size is 10 × 10, the obstacles are represented by black squares, the triangles represent the starting points, and the red circle represent the target points, and the blue circle represents the current position. The planned path is represented by a green line. The maximum speed of the ferry is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm135" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the maximum angular velocity is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm136" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>30</mn><mo> </mo><mi>deg</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the maximum acceleration is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm137" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>0.3</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the maximum angular acceleration is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm138" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>deg</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the velocity resolution is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm139" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>0.01</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the angular velocity resolution is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm140" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>deg</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the unit forward simulation time is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm141" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, and the time resolution is <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm142" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>0.1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>. The above parameters of the ferry vehicle are consistent in the following simulation environment: Through the simulation experiment, it was found that the traditional dynamic window method has the following problems:</p>
<p>When the heading angle weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm143" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> is larger, the obstacle distance weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm144" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> is smaller, and the speed weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm145" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> is smaller, the ferry vehicle will be more inclined to move towards the target point, but because it does not pay enough attention to obstacle avoidance, when the obstacle is on the line between the ferry vehicle and the target point, the vehicle cannot get around the obstacle very well, as shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f013" class="usa-link">Figure 13</a>. At the same time, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm146" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.8</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm147" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>β</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm148" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f013"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 13.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g013.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/a5fbab64c6f6/sensors-24-04041-g013.jpg" loading="lazy" height="488" width="603" alt="Figure 13"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f013/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Scene one.</p></figcaption></figure><p>When the distance weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm149" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> of the obstacle is larger and the heading angle weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm150" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and the speed weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm151" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> are smaller, the ferry vehicle will be more inclined to bypass the obstacle. But this may cause the ferry vehicle to avoid the obstacles prematurely, thus preventing the vehicle from passing through the free space between the two obstacles, choosing to bypass the obstacles and increasing the length of the path, as shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f014" class="usa-link">Figure 14</a>; at the same time, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm152" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm153" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>β</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.8</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm154" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f014/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>

As shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f015" class="usa-link">Figure 15</a>, at this time, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm158" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm159" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>β</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm160" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>γ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.8</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f015/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Scene three.</p></figcaption></figure><p>Thus, it can be seen that in different scenarios, the traditional dynamic window method cannot successfully complete the planning task because of its poor adaptability to the environment because of the fixed weight coefficient.</p></section></section><section id="sec4dot2-sensors-24-04041"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">4.2. Improved DWA</h3>
<p>Through the theoretical research and experimental verification of the traditional DWA, it can be found that the working environment of the DWA is diverse, and it is difficult to determine a set of appropriate fixed weight coefficients. The fixed weight combination in different scenes will seriously affect the path planning effect of the traditional DWA and may lead to problems such as the ferry vehicle not being able to avoid obstacles, the path being too long or missing the target point, and so on. In order to improve the above problems, this paper introduces fuzzy control theory to improve the traditional DWA to make it output the weight coefficient of the evaluation function adaptively according to the actual situation and improve the adaptability of the algorithm to the complex environment.</p>
<section id="sec4dot2dot1-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.2.1. Basic Theory of Fuzzy Control</h4>
<p>Fuzzy control fuzzifies the input, and then inference and output through fuzzy rules, so as to realize the control of the system. The fuzzy control system has strong anti-interference ability and robustness, and the control effect is less affected by the change of parameters. At the same time, in the process of application, it is not required to establish an accurate mathematical model for the controlled object, and the control strategy is relatively simple and convenient for application [<a href="#B25-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B25-sensors-24-04041">25</a>].</p>
<p>The design of a fuzzy controller mainly includes four parts: input fuzzification, establishment of fuzzy control rules, fuzzy inference, and defuzzification, of which the core part is the establishment of fuzzy control rules. The design principle of the fuzzy controller framework is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f016" class="usa-link">Figure 16</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f016"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 16.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g016.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/783ffd3246aa/sensors-24-04041-g016.jpg" loading="lazy" height="260" width="750" alt="Figure 16"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f016/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Fuzzy controller framework.</p></figcaption></figure></section><section id="sec4dot2dot2-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.2.2. Design of a DWA Based on Fuzzy Control</h4>
<p>According to the characteristics of the DWA and the basic principle of the fuzzy controller, we can know that the ideal output value can be obtained when selecting the appropriate input value, selecting the reasonable membership function, and formulating the appropriate fuzzy control rules. The effect of path planning in the traditional DWA mainly depends on three weight coefficients. Therefore, this paper uses fuzzy control theory, considers the directionality principle and safety principle in path planning, and introduces the weight coefficient to design three fuzzy controllers, respectively, to realize the dynamic adjustment of the three weight coefficients of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm161" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm162" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm163" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>. In order to improve the adaptability of the ferry in a dynamic environment.</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none"><li>
<span class="label">(1)</span><p class="display-inline">Fuzzification</p>
</li></ul>
<p>In the fuzzy controller designed in this section, the inputs involved are the distance between the ferry vehicle and the obstacle <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm164" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>; the distance between the ferry vehicle and the target point <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm165" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>; the heading angle of the ferry vehicle relative to the target point <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm166" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>; the output includes the three weight coefficients of the DWA <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm167" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm168" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm169" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>; and the fuzzy rule weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm170" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span>. The fuzzy definition of input and output is as follows: The fuzzy set of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm171" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> is defined as {near (N), medium (M), far (F)}, and the domain is [0, 2]. When <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm172" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>0.5</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, it is fuzzified to N; when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm173" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, it is fuzzified to M; and when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm174" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>1.5</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, it is fuzzified to F. The fuzzy set of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm175" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> is {near (N), middle (M), far (F)}, and the domain is [0, 4]. When <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm176" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, it is fuzzified to N; when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm177" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, to M; when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm178" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, to F. The unit of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm179" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> is degree (deg), the fuzzy set is {negative large (XS), negative (S), medium (M), positive (B), positive large (XB)}, and the domain is [−180, 180]. When <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm180" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≤</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>90</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the fuzzy description is negative large (XS); when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm181" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>≥</mo><mn>90</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the fuzzy description is positive large (XB). When <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm182" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>45</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, fuzzy description is negative (S); when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm183" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, fuzzy description is positive (B); and when <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm184" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mrow></math></span>, fuzzy description is medium (M). <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f017" class="usa-link">Figure 17</a> shows the membership functions of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm185" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm186" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm187" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f017"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 17.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g017.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/951e93b439f7/sensors-24-04041-g017.jpg" loading="lazy" height="402" width="762" alt="Figure 17"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f017/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p><span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm248" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm249" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm250" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> membership function. (<strong>a</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm251" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> membership function; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm252" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> membership function; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm253" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> membership function.</p></figcaption></figure><p>For the output of the fuzzy controller <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm188" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm189" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm190" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>, the fuzzy set is {XS, small (S), medium (M), large (L), and maximum (XL)}, and the domain is [0, 1]. For the output of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm191" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>, the domain is [0, 1]. The fuzzy set is {small (S), medium (M), large (L)}, as shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f018" class="usa-link">Figure 18</a>, is the membership function of the output.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f018"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 18.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g018.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/b7b0cfa3621a/sensors-24-04041-g018.jpg" loading="lazy" height="210" width="796" alt="Figure 18"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f018/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Membership function of output. (<strong>a</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm254" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm255" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm256" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span> membership function; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm257" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> membership function.</p></figcaption></figure><ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none"><li>
<span class="label">(2)</span><p class="display-inline">The formulation of fuzzy rules</p>
</li></ul>
<p>Fuzzy rules are the core of fuzzy controllers [<a href="#B26-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B26-sensors-24-04041">26</a>]. According to the analysis of the traditional DWA, the adjustment of the weight coefficient of the DWA is actually a trade-off between the direction, safety, and speed of the ferry vehicle according to the current environment, so as to find out the most suitable speed trajectory for the current target. Among the three principles, the driving speed will have an impact on the algorithm efficiency of both directivity and safety principles. Therefore, this paper first designs two fuzzy controllers considering the directionality principle and the safety principle, and then designs a fuzzy controller that can output the weight coefficients of the directivity and safety principles, thus obtaining the weight coefficients of different evaluation factors in the evaluation function of the DWA. The specific fuzzy rules are designed as follows:</p>
<p>The factors that affect the directionality principle are the distance <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm192" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> between the ferry vehicle and the target point and the navigation angle <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm193" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> with the target point, so the structure of the directional fuzzy controller <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm194" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f019" class="usa-link">Figure 19</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f019"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 19.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g019.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/872020d201af/sensors-24-04041-g019.jpg" loading="lazy" height="273" width="638" alt="Figure 19"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f019/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Directional fuzzy controller.</p></figcaption></figure><p>When the ferry vehicle approaches the target point and the heading angle is large, the direction should be given priority, a larger value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm195" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> should be used, and the speed should be reduced to a smaller value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm196" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>. When the distance between the ferry vehicle and the target point is long, and the heading angle is small, priority should be given to increasing the driving speed, using a larger value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm197" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>, and choosing a moderate value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm198" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> to keep moving in the direction of the target. When it is far away from the target point, priority is given to increasing the speed and then gradually maintaining the heading angle close to the target point. When approaching the target point, gradually reduce the speed and give priority to the direction. The specific fuzzy rules are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-t005" class="usa-link">Table 5</a>.</p>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="table/sensors-24-04041-t005/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div></section><p>After the fuzzy vector under the directional principle is obtained by the Mamdani type reasoning method [<a href="#B27-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B27-sensors-24-04041">27</a>], the clear output control quantity is obtained by defuzzifying by the center of gravity method, and the accurate values of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm199" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm200" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> can be obtained. At the same time, in order to take into account for the security, the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm201" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>β</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is 0.2.</p>
<p>The factors affecting the safety principle are the distance <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm202" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> between the ferry vehicle and the obstacle and the distance <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm203" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> from the target point, so the structure of the safety fuzzy controller <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm204" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f020" class="usa-link">Figure 20</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f020"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 20.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g020.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/bcce6081a0e1/sensors-24-04041-g020.jpg" loading="lazy" height="257" width="603" alt="Figure 20"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f020/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Safety fuzzy controller.</p></figcaption></figure><p>When the distance between the ferry vehicle and the obstacle and the target point is very small, the speed should be reduced first and the direction should be given priority. When the ferry vehicle is close to the target point and far from the obstacle, it should appropriately increase the speed, reduce the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm205" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and give priority to approaching the target point. When the ferry vehicle is far from the obstacle and the target point, the ferry vehicle gives priority to speeding up close to the target point, and the requirement for obstacle avoidance is reduced. When the ferry vehicle is far away from the target point but close to the obstacle, we should give priority to avoiding obstacles, take a larger value for <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm206" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and reduce the speed by <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm207" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> value appropriately. The specific security fuzzy rules are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-t006" class="usa-link">Table 6</a>. The output is defuzzified by the center of gravity method, and the exact values of output <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm208" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm209" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> under the safety principle are obtained. At the same time, in order to take into account the directional principle, the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm210" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>α</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is 0.2.</p>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="table/sensors-24-04041-t006/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div></section><p>Because different environments attach different importance to the directionality principle and safety principle, it is necessary to consider the distance <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm211" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> between the ferry vehicle and the obstacle and the heading angle <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm212" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span> with the target point and introduce the fuzzy rule weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm213" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span> of directionality and safety. By combining the output of the fuzzy controller obtained from <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm214" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm215" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span>, the final expressions of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm216" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm217" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm218" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> are obtained:</p>
<table class="disp-formula p" id="FD24-sensors-24-04041"><tr>
<td class="formula"><math id="mm219" display="block" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>α</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>β</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>γ</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>ε</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msub><mi>α</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>β</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>γ</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>ε</mi><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msub><mi>α</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>β</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>γ</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></math></td>
<td class="label">(24)</td>
</tr></table>
<p>The fusion fuzzy controller <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm220" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is designed, and the structure of the fuzzy controller <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm221" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span> is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f021" class="usa-link">Figure 21</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f021"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 21.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g021.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/9d7f60753ec0/sensors-24-04041-g021.jpg" loading="lazy" height="259" width="604" alt="Figure 21"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f021/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Fusion fuzzy controller.</p></figcaption></figure><p>When the ferry vehicle is far away from the obstacle and the heading angle is large, we should give priority to the directional principle and take a smaller value of the weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm222" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span>. When the heading angle of the ferry vehicle is small and close to the obstacle, we should give priority to the safety principle and take a larger value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm223" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span>. The closer the ferry vehicle is to the obstacle, the more attention that should be paid to safety, and the greater the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm224" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span>. The larger the heading angle of the ferry vehicle is, the higher the emphasis on directionality should be, and the smaller the value of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm225" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span> should be. When the emphasis on directivity and safety is close, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm226" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>ε</mi></mrow></math></span> should take the appropriate value. The output is defuzzified by the center of gravity method. The specific fusion fuzzy rules are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-t007" class="usa-link">Table 7</a>.</p>
<section class="tw xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-t007"><h5 class="obj_head">Table 7.</h5>
<div class="caption p"><p>Fusion fuzzy rule table.</p></div>
<div class="tbl-box p" tabindex="0"><table class="content" frame="hsides" rules="groups">
<thead><tr>
