<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g022.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/f3692e7e0c60/sensors-24-04041-g022.jpg" loading="lazy" height="609" width="756" alt="Figure 22"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f022/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Fuzzy regular three-dimensional surface graph. (<strong>a</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm258" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm259" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm260" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure></section><section id="sec4dot2dot3-sensors-24-04041"><h4 class="pmc_sec_title">4.2.3. Simulation Experiment of the DWA Algorithm Based on Fuzzy Control</h4>
<p>In order to verify that the improved dynamic window algorithm can better adapt to the complex environment than the traditional dynamic window algorithm, the DWA algorithm based on fuzzy control is used to simulate the same environment as in <a href="#sec4dot1dot4-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link">Section 4.1.4</a>. The starting point and target point of the experiment and the parameters of the ferry vehicle are consistent with the traditional dynamic window method. The simulation results in this section are compared with those in <a href="#sec4dot1dot4-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link">Section 4.1.4</a>.</p>
<p>In the first scene, the simulation results are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f023" class="usa-link">Figure 23</a>, from the path results and the weight coefficient variation curve of the evaluation function, it can be seen that when it is close to 100 steps, the ferry vehicle is close to the obstacle, but there is still a long distance from the target point, so safety is given priority. The <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm230" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> value of the output of the fuzzy controller decreases, and the <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm231" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> value increases. Comply with the fuzzy rules set out in <a href="#sec4dot2dot2-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link">Section 4.2.2</a>. At the same time, as the ferry vehicle is closer to the target point and the obstacles around the target point, the smaller the speed weight <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm232" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> is, the greater the values of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm233" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm234" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> are. Compared with the path simulated by the traditional DWA in scene 1 in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f013" class="usa-link">Figure 13</a>, the ferry vehicle can dynamically adjust the avoidance weight according to the actual distance from the obstacle on the basis of ensuring the direction so that the ferry vehicle can smoothly bypass the obstacle in front of the target point and reach the target point, so as to avoid a situation where the traditional DWA crosses the obstacle in order to reach the target point faster.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f023"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 23.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g023.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/fd49b4514f47/sensors-24-04041-g023.jpg" loading="lazy" height="582" width="701" alt="Figure 23"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f023/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>The simulation results of fuzzy DWA in scene 1. (<strong>a</strong>) Path; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm261" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm262" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>d</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm263" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The simulation is carried out in scene 2, the simulation results are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f024" class="usa-link">Figure 24</a>, and the simulation results show that when there are both obstacles and drivable channels in the direction of the current position of the ferry vehicle facing the target point, that is, the position in the figure is close to 80 steps, the ferry vehicle is close to the obstacle, there is still a moderate distance from the target point, the values of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm235" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm236" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> output by the fuzzy controller decrease, and the ferry vehicle chooses the path between the two obstacles. Finally, reach the target point. Avoid the situation where the traditional DWA gives up the optimal path in order to avoid obstacles. The distance and quality of the planned path are effectively optimized.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f024"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 24.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g024.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/03a7eb6265dc/sensors-24-04041-g024.jpg" loading="lazy" height="655" width="782" alt="Figure 24"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f024/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>The simulation results of fuzzy DWA in scene 2. (<strong>a</strong>) Path; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm264" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm265" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>d</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm266" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The simulation is carried out in scene 3, the simulation results are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f025" class="usa-link">Figure 25</a>, and it can be seen from the simulation results that when the ferry vehicle is getting closer and closer to the target point, the speed weight <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm237" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> of the fuzzy controller will be greatly reduced, the speed of the ferry vehicle will slow down, and the relative heading angle weight will become a moderate value. The weight of avoiding obstacles is increased so that the ferry vehicle can reach the target point smoothly and safely. It will not happen that the ferry bus misses the target point because of the constant speed weight of the traditional DWA.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f025"><h5 class="obj_head">Figure 25.</h5>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g025.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/16a3e329a27d/sensors-24-04041-g025.jpg" loading="lazy" height="648" width="732" alt="Figure 25"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f025/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>The simulation results of fuzzy DWA in scene 3. (<strong>a</strong>) Path; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm267" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm268" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>d</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm269" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure><p>Through the simulation experiment of the DWA based on fuzzy control, it is obvious that, compared with the traditional DWA, the improved algorithm solves various problems caused by the constant weight coefficient of the evaluation function or the excessive weight coefficient of a certain weight. It solves the problem that the traditional dynamic window method cannot bypass the obstacles or pass through the narrow channel, and thus misses the target point. At the same time, it effectively improves the quality of the planning path.</p></section></section></section><section id="sec5-sensors-24-04041"><h2 class="pmc_sec_title">5. A Fusion Algorithm Based on the Improved A* Algorithm and Fuzzy Control DWA </h2>
<section id="sec5dot1-sensors-24-04041"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">5.1. Algorithm Fusion Principle</h3>
<p>The improved A* algorithm can plan the global optimal path in the static scene where the obstacles are known, but it cannot avoid the unknown obstacles in the static scene. The improved DWA can avoid temporary unknown obstacles in real time, but in a large-scale environment with a large number of obstacles, due to the lack of guidance from the intermediate point, it is easy to cause the planning results to fall into local optimization. It is not difficult to find that the integration of global and local planning algorithms can make up for each other’s shortcomings. The key nodes of the path planned by the improved A* algorithm are used as sub-target punctuation points in the local planning of the improved DWA to guide the local path planning. The combination of the two can not only retain global optimality but also avoid unknown obstacles [<a href="#B28-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B28-sensors-24-04041">28</a>]. The specific flow chart of the algorithm fusion is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f026" class="usa-link">Figure 26</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f026"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 26.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><img class="graphic" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/680c50a566fe/sensors-24-04041-g026.jpg" loading="lazy" height="1654" width="684" alt="Figure 26"></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f026/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Algorithm fusion flow chart.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The specific implementation steps of algorithm fusion are as follows:</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none">
<li>
<span class="label">(1)</span><p class="display-inline">The ferry vehicle uses sensors to obtain information about the surrounding environmental obstacles and converts it into environmental raster maps that can be used for path planning.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(2)</span><p class="display-inline">The improved A* algorithm is used to plan the optimal global path and smooth the path.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(3)</span><p class="display-inline">Take a key node at the same distance on the planned global path, record the coordinates of all the key nodes, and take them as the sub-target punctuation of the local planning.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(4)</span><p class="display-inline">According to the acquired local sub-target punctuation, the fuzzy control DWA is used to plan the local path, the surrounding environment information is sensed in real time by the sensors on the ferry vehicle, and the grid map is updated in real time when there are unknown obstacles. Thus, a local path that avoids obstacles can be planned. When the obstacle is bypassed, the ferry vehicle will first return to the global path to continue. If the current local sub-target punctuation is reached, then switch to the next local sub-target punctuation.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(5)</span><p class="display-inline">Determine whether the currently arriving local sub-target point is a global target point; if not, jump to step 4. Continue the cycle until the ferry reaches the global target point and the algorithm is over.</p>
</li>
</ul></section><section id="sec5dot2-sensors-24-04041"><h3 class="pmc_sec_title">5.2. Simulation Experiment and Result Analysis of the Fusion Algorithm</h3>
<p>In order to verify the effectiveness of the fusion algorithm designed in this paper in different environments, considering that the obstacles encountered by ferry vehicles in the process of path planning can be divided into two categories, one is the known static obstacles in the planning environment. Second, there are unknown static obstacles and dynamic obstacles on the basis of the known environment [<a href="#B29-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B29-sensors-24-04041">29</a>]. According to the above situation, two groups of simulation experiments are carried out on the algorithm proposed in this paper. The parameters of the ferry vehicle in the experiment are consistent with the traditional dynamic window method. The two sets of simulations of the path planning of the fusion algorithm use the environment map shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f027" class="usa-link">Figure 27</a>a.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f027"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 27.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g027.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/8f58064ae074/sensors-24-04041-g027.jpg" loading="lazy" height="282" width="706" alt="Figure 27"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f027/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Simulation results of the fusion algorithm in a known static environment. (<strong>a</strong>) Environmental map; (<strong>b</strong>) Simulation results of fusion algorithm.</p></figcaption></figure><p>In the first group of simulation environments, there are known static obstacles. The starting point and target point of the ferry vehicle are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f027" class="usa-link">Figure 27</a>a, and the simulation results are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f027" class="usa-link">Figure 27</a>b. It can be seen from the figure that the fusion algorithm uses the key nodes of the global path planned by the improved A* algorithm as the local sub-target points of the improved dynamic window method, guides the improved dynamic window method along the global path, successfully bypasses the obstacles, and finally reaches the target point; the path is relatively smooth. However, the paths planned by the traditional DWA and the traditional A* algorithm are too close to or even colliding with the obstacles. It can be seen that it is very necessary to use the fusion algorithm designed in this paper for path planning.</p>
<p>The second group of simulation experimental environments simulates that the ferry vehicle plans the global optimal path under the known static obstacle environment, and there are unknown static and dynamic obstacles on the way along the global path. As shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f028" class="usa-link">Figure 28</a>a, the coordinates of the two unknown static obstacles are (8.5, 15.5) and (14.5, 3.5), respectively, represented by a gray grid. The dynamic obstacle moves from (12.5) to (9.5) at the speed of <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm238" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mrow><mn>0.05</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mrow></math></span>, which is represented by a red grid, and the trajectory of the dynamic obstacle is represented by a black dotted line. The prediction trajectory of the fusion algorithm is represented by a green curve. As can be seen from <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f028" class="usa-link">Figure 28</a>, at (a) moment, the ferry vehicle increases the corner to bypass the first static obstacle; at (b) moment, the ferry vehicle adjusts its driving direction after sensing the dynamic obstacle; at (c) moment, the ferry vehicle successfully bypasses the dynamic obstacle and drives to a new route; and at (d) moment, the ferry vehicle bypasses the second static obstacle and reaches the finish line smoothly.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f028"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 28.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g028.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/406e28f87de3/sensors-24-04041-g028.jpg" loading="lazy" height="590" width="720" alt="Figure 28"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f028/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Simulation results of the fusion algorithm in an environment with unknown dynamic and static obstacles. (<strong>a</strong>) Bypass the first static obstacle. (<strong>b</strong>) Encounter dynamic obstacles. (<strong>c</strong>) Bypass dynamic obstacles. (<strong>d</strong>) Bypass the second static obstacle and reach the target point.</p></figcaption></figure><p>During the operation of the fusion algorithm, the changes in the three weight coefficients output by the fuzzy controller are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f029" class="usa-link">Figure 29</a>, and the linear velocity and angular velocity variation curves of the ferry vehicle are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f030" class="usa-link">Figure 30</a>. Every 0.1 of linear velocity represents 0.4 m/s, and every 0.1 of angular velocity represents 6 deg/s. When the program iteration starts with 50 steps, the ferry vehicle encounters unknown static obstacles. At this time, the speed weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm239" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> decreases, the obstacle distance weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm240" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> increases, the heading angle weight coefficient <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm241" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> basically remains stable, and the ferry vehicle tends to slow down and avoid obstacles. The corresponding ferry vehicle maintains the frontline speed to avoid obstacles smoothly, and the angular velocity increases. When the procedure iterates to 120 steps, the ferry vehicle meets the dynamic obstacle at first, <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm242" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm243" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> are in the large value, and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm244" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> is in the middle value; that is, the ferry vehicle tends to drive faster towards the target point, and the weight of obstacle avoidance is relatively low. But as the dynamic obstacle approaches the ferry vehicle, the values <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm245" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span> and <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm246" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span> begin to decrease greatly, and the value <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm247" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span> increases greatly. The primary task of the ferry vehicle is to avoid the dynamic obstacle; the speed and heading angle weight decrease, and the corresponding speed of the ferry vehicle decreases. At the same time, the angular velocity increases when turning away from the target and avoiding the obstacle. In addition, in the process of avoiding dynamic obstacles, the three weight coefficients change violently according to the relative relationship between ferry vehicles and dynamic obstacles. When approaching the target point, the heading angle weight coefficient becomes larger, the speed weight coefficient decreases, the obstacle weight coefficient is moderate, and the three coefficients are maintained in a relatively stable range. The ferry vehicle successfully avoids the second unknown static obstacle and reaches the target point.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f029"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 29.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g029.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/19a1653a29d4/sensors-24-04041-g029.jpg" loading="lazy" height="645" width="780" alt="Figure 29"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f029/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Change of weight coefficient of the fusion algorithm in the environment with unknown dynamic and static obstacles. (<strong>a</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm270" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm271" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm272" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure><figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f030"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 30.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g030.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/c78fc46bfd7c/sensors-24-04041-g030.jpg" loading="lazy" height="323" width="770" alt="Figure 30"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f030/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Variation in linear velocity and angular velocity of ferry vehicles. (<strong>a</strong>) Linear velocity variation curve; (<strong>b</strong>) angular velocity variation curve.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The above two sets of simulation experiments verify the effectiveness of the fusion algorithm when there are no unknown obstacles and unknown dynamic and static obstacles in the same map. In order to further verify the adaptability of the algorithm under different maps [<a href="#B30-sensors-24-04041" class="usa-link" aria-describedby="B30-sensors-24-04041">30</a>], a more complex 30 × 30 environment map is selected for the simulation experiment. The environmental map is also divided into two cases: no unknown obstacles and unknown dynamic and static obstacles. The experimental results are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f031"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 31.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g031.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/26bbd547eedf/sensors-24-04041-g031.jpg" loading="lazy" height="628" width="764" alt="Figure 31"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f031/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Simulation experiment results of a 30 × 30 environment map. (<strong>a</strong>) Environment without unknown obstacles. (<strong>b</strong>) Encounter dynamic obstacles and turn. (<strong>c</strong>) Successfully circumvent dynamic obstacles. (<strong>d</strong>) Move the static obstacle around and reach the target point.</p></figcaption></figure><p>In the environment without unknown obstacles in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>a, the fusion algorithm can plan a complete path according to the key nodes in the global path of the improved A* algorithm. Compared with the global path planned by the A* algorithm, the distance between the path and obstacles planned by the fusion algorithm is safer, the path smoothness is higher, and it is more suitable for ferry vehicle tracking. <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>b–d adds unknown dynamic and static obstacles to the environment of <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>a. As can be seen from the map, the path planned by the improved A* algorithm cannot avoid unknown obstacles. In addition, through experiments, the traditional DWA cannot successfully plan a complete path from the starting point to the target point on such a complex map. Through <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>b,c, it can be seen that the ferry vehicle can adjust its driving route in time and bypass the dynamic obstacles when it encounters them. At the same time, it can quickly return to the original path according to the key points of the improved A* algorithm after the danger is lifted. As can be seen from <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f031" class="usa-link">Figure 31</a>d, the path planned by the fusion algorithm can successfully bypass two unknown static obstacles and reach the target point. The whole path is safe and reliable. The changes in the three weight coefficients of the fuzzy controller output during the operation of the fusion algorithm are shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f032" class="usa-link">Figure 32</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f032"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 32.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g032.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/6cfcb10875db/sensors-24-04041-g032.jpg" loading="lazy" height="635" width="754" alt="Figure 32"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f032/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>The change in output weight coefficient in the presence of unknown dynamic and static obstacles. (<strong>a</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm273" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>α</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>b</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm274" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>β</mi></mrow></math></span>; (<strong>c</strong>) <span xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><math id="mm275" overflow="linebreak"><mrow><mi>γ</mi></mrow></math></span>.</p></figcaption></figure><p>In order to verify whether the path planned by the fusion algorithm has sufficient advantages, in the environment of the above two different environmental maps and dynamic and static obstacles, the simulation experiments are carried out by using the Dijkstra algorithm, ant colony algorithm, and traditional A* algorithm, respectively, and the obtained path is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-f033" class="usa-link">Figure 33</a>.</p>
<figure class="fig xbox font-sm" id="sensors-24-04041-f033"><h4 class="obj_head">Figure 33.</h4>
<p class="img-box line-height-none margin-x-neg-2 tablet:margin-x-0 text-center"><a class="tileshop" target="_blank" href="https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/core/lw/2.0/html/tileshop_pmc/tileshop_pmc_inline.html?title=Click%20on%20image%20to%20zoom&amp;p=PMC3&amp;id=11244110_sensors-24-04041-g033.jpg"><img class="graphic zoom-in" src="https://cdn.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/blobs/cb44/11244110/adc8b547bd03/sensors-24-04041-g033.jpg" loading="lazy" height="285" width="724" alt="Figure 33"></a></p>
<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="figure/sensors-24-04041-f033/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div>
<figcaption><p>Simulation and comparison results of different algorithms. (<strong>a</strong>) 20 × 20 environment map; (<strong>b</strong>) 30 × 30 environment map.</p></figcaption></figure><p>The performance comparison of the four algorithms is shown in <a href="#sensors-24-04041-t008" class="usa-link">Table 8</a>. It can be seen that in addition to the fusion algorithm, the remaining three traditional algorithms plan the risk path of collision in the face of unknown dynamic and static obstacles, and only the fusion algorithm can plan a safe collision-free path. And in terms of the number of path turns, path length, and algorithm time, compared with other traditional algorithms, the path length planned by the fusion algorithm is shorter, the number of turns is lower, and the path is smoother. It is more suitable for ferry vehicles to follow. Moreover, the algorithm takes less time and has higher efficiency, which fully proves that the improved fusion algorithm is effective.</p>

<div class="p text-right font-secondary"><a href="table/sensors-24-04041-t008/" class="usa-link" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Open in a new tab</a></div></section><p>Through the above experiments on the distribution of different obstacles under the same map, it is proved that the improved fusion algorithm is effective in the face of unknown dynamic and static obstacles, and by improving the size and complexity of the map, the adaptability of the fusion algorithm in different scenes is verified; compared with several traditional algorithms, the performance of the fusion algorithm is superior to the common traditional algorithms.</p>
<p>According to the above experiments, the fusion algorithm solves problems that traditional algorithms cannot, such as the A* algorithm’s inability to avoid unknown obstacles and the DWA easily falling into local optimization in a large area environment, and the effectiveness and adaptability of the algorithm are verified.</p></section></section><section id="sec6-sensors-24-04041"><h2 class="pmc_sec_title">6. Conclusions</h2>
<p>This paper presents a path planning algorithm based on the fusion of the improved A* and DWA algorithms for driverless ferry vehicles. In order to solve the traditional A* algorithm, the search direction is not high, the search time is long, the path is not smooth, and it is only suitable for static maps and other problems.</p>
<ul class="list" style="list-style-type:none">
<li>
<span class="label">(1)</span><p class="display-inline">In the aspect of global planning, the vector angle cosine value is introduced to improve the heuristic function on the basis of the traditional A* algorithm, which reduces the inspection of useless nodes and makes the path planning more directional. In the traditional A* algorithm, the search neighborhood of 3 × 3 is extended to the search neighborhood of 9 × 9, which shortens the search time, improves the search efficiency, and optimizes the path length. Then the path is smoothed based on a quasi-uniform cubic B-spline curve, which makes the planned path more suitable for ferry vehicle tracking. The effectiveness of the improved algorithm is verified by simulation experiments.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(2)</span><p class="display-inline">In the aspect of local planning, the problems caused by the traditional DWA algorithm using constant weight coefficients in different environments are analyzed through simulation experiments. Fuzzy control theory is introduced to improve the traditional DWA algorithm. Based on the principles of directionality and safety, three fuzzy controllers are designed to integrate the two principles to realize the dynamic adjustment of the three weight coefficients of the trajectory evaluation function in different environments. Then the simulation results show that the improved DWA algorithm can solve the problems of the traditional DWA algorithm, such as being unable to bypass obstacles, unable to pass through narrow channels, and missing target points. It effectively improves the quality of the planning path.</p>
</li>
<li>
<span class="label">(3)</span><p class="display-inline">In the aspect of algorithm fusion, in order to solve the problem that the global planning algorithm cannot avoid unknown obstacles and the local planning algorithm is easy to fall into local optimization because of the lack of intermediate point guidance in large-scale environments, a fusion algorithm based on an improved A* algorithm and a fuzzy control DWA algorithm is proposed. The simulation results show that the path planned by the fusion algorithm not only fits the global static optimal path but can also effectively avoid the unknown dynamic and static obstacles in the path. At the same time, it still has a good effect on obstacle avoidance planning in the more complex environmental map, which fully verifies the effectiveness and adaptability of the fusion algorithm in ferry vehicle path planning.</p>
</li>
</ul>
<p>Overall, this paper provides a feasible and effective solution to improve the path planning performance of driverless ferry vehicles, which has potential significance for the application of driverless ferry vehicles.</p>
<p>In view of the fact that the improved DWA algorithm based on fuzzy control depends on subjective experience in formulating fuzzy rules, it is sometimes necessary to adjust the fuzzy rules according to the empirical changes in the actual verification process. In the next research, we can combine the deep learning algorithm to deeply optimize the fuzzy rules of the weight coefficient of the DWA algorithm to realize the adaptive adjustment of the fuzzy rules, so as to further increase the adaptability of the algorithm. At the same time, the study of this paper only aims at a single ferry vehicle, which has limitations, and more in-depth research can be carried out from the perspective of multi-ferry vehicle formation path planning in the future.</p></section><section id="notes1"><h2 class="pmc_sec_title">Author Contributions</h2>
